/// Bind a rule expression over a table's focal row.
pub fn bind_rule(catalog: &Catalog, info: &TableInfo, rule: &RuleDecl) -> Fallible<RuleExpr> {
    let bindings = RuleBindings { old: rule.bindings.first().cloned(), next: rule.bindings.get(1).cloned(), update: rule.effect == Effect::Update, insert: rule.effect == Effect::Insert, hops: rule.effect != Effect::Read, present: Vec::new() };
    rule_expr(catalog, info, &rule.value, &bindings).map_err(|error| {
        let mut error = super::first_paste::inherited_rule(catalog, info, rule).unwrap_or(error);
        if error.code == "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR" && rule.effect == Effect::Update && rule.bindings.is_empty()
            && error.rewrite == "write this as a named transition" {
            let source = super::rule_operator::named_update_source(&rule.value);
            error.rewrite = format!("name both row images: `{source}`");
            error.source_rewrite = Some(source);
            error.source_rewrite_kind = Some(crate::diag::SourceRewriteKind::NamedUpdate);
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.036#13.6 — the named form of a whole unnamed update rule, so the rewrite keeps every other clause.
        if error.code == "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR" && rule.effect == Effect::Update && rule.bindings.is_empty() && error.rewrite.starts_with("name both images") {
            let comparison = error.message.split('`').nth(1).unwrap_or_default().to_owned();
            error.rewrite = super::rule_operator::named_update(&info.decl.name, &rule.value, &comparison);
        }
        if error.code == "E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP" {
            error.rewrite = match rule.effect {
                Effect::Read => reference::read_hop_rewrite(catalog, info, &rule.value).unwrap_or_else(|| "a read rule probes one hop: copy the deciding value onto this row (immutable, checked against the parent by the insert rule), or use read through .parent".into()),
                _ => reference::reference_rewrite(catalog, info, &rule.value),
            };
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.036#14.3 — a read-through child binds its parent's rule over hidden
        // captures (`.$enrollment.dancer`): its refusal is the parent's (`internal_names` drops the copy).
        // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.6 — a `read through` that did not expand (E_READ_THROUGH says why)
        // leaves its internal call; its refusal is that problem's cascade, spelled as written.
        if let Expr::Call { name, args, .. } = &rule.value && matches!(name.as_str(), "$readThrough" | "$readThroughLive") {
            let reference = match args.as_slice() { [Expr::Name { name, .. }] => name.clone(), _ => "parent".into() };
            error.message = format!("`read through .{reference}` did not expand, so {} has no read rule yet", info.decl.name);
            // The cascade convention: `check` hides it beside its cause unless --verbose.
            error.rewrite = "fix this Quarry line and rerun snapback4 check".into();
            error.cascade_of = Some(rule.site.clone());
            error.mechanical = false;
            return error;
        }
        if rule.effect == Effect::Read && let Some(through) = &info.through {
            error.rewrite = format!("repair {}'s read rule (its own diagnostic prints the repair); {} reads through it", through.parent, info.decl.name);
            error.cascade_of = Some(error.site.clone());
        }
        error
    })
}

#[derive(Clone)]
struct RuleBindings {
    old: Option<String>,
    next: Option<String>,
    update: bool,
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum Effect {
    Read,
    Insert,
    Update,
    Delete,
}

impl fmt::Display for Effect {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(match self {
            Self::Read => "read",
            Self::Insert => "insert",
            Self::Update => "update",
            Self::Delete => "delete",
        })
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum RuleExpr {
    Public { reason: String },
    Allow,
    Deny,
    Eq(RuleTerm, RuleTerm),
    Ne(RuleTerm, RuleTerm),
    /// Ordered comparisons are confined to named update transitions and to
    /// insert rules over the row's own required int/time columns.
    Compare(crate::ir::BinaryOp, RuleTerm, RuleTerm),
    And(Vec<RuleExpr>),
    Or(Vec<RuleExpr>),
    Not(Box<RuleExpr>),
    Exists {
        table: String, index: String, key: Vec<RuleTerm>, provenance: KeyProvenance,
        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
        equalities: Vec<(String, RuleTerm)>,
    },
    /// True only when the indexed target row was inserted earlier in this
    /// same transaction; insert rules only.
    Created {
        table: String, index: String, key: Vec<RuleTerm>, provenance: KeyProvenance,
        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
        equalities: Vec<(String, RuleTerm)>,
    },
}

impl RuleExpr {
    pub fn is_public(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::Public { .. })
    }

    pub fn is_uniformly_readable(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::Public { .. } | Self::Allow)
    }

    /// Every `.field` the rule reads, on any probe key or comparison.
    pub fn fields(&self, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        let term = |term: &RuleTerm, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>| {
            if let RuleTerm::Field(field) | RuleTerm::Reference { column: field, .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { column: field, .. } = term {
                out.insert(field.clone());
            }
        };
        match self {
            Self::Public { .. } | Self::Allow | Self::Deny => {}
            Self::Eq(left, right) | Self::Ne(left, right) | Self::Compare(_, left, right) => {
                term(left, out);
                term(right, out);
            }
            Self::And(items) | Self::Or(items) => items.iter().for_each(|item| item.fields(out)),
            Self::Not(inner) => inner.fields(out),
            Self::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | Self::Created { key, equalities, .. } => {
                key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_, term)| term)).for_each(|item| term(item, out));
            }
        }
    }

    /// The disjuncts of the rule: an `or` flattens, anything else is itself.
    pub fn disjuncts(&self) -> Vec<&RuleExpr> {
        match self {
            Self::Or(items) => items.iter().flat_map(|item| item.disjuncts()).collect(),
            other => vec![other],
        }
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.6 — service seats read on the server and never enlarge a device's audience.
    pub fn service_grant(&self) -> Option<&str> {
        match self {
            Self::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Id(principal)))
            | Self::Eq(RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Id(principal)), RuleTerm::Viewer)
                if principal.strip_prefix("service:").is_some_and(|name| !name.is_empty()) => Some(principal),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// Remove only entire service disjuncts; a conjunct, negation or ordinary
    /// principal equality must still pass the ordinary exact-audience check.
    pub fn device_audience_rule(&self) -> std::borrow::Cow<'_, RuleExpr> {
        let terms = self.disjuncts();
        if !terms.iter().any(|term| term.service_grant().is_some()) { return std::borrow::Cow::Borrowed(self); }
        let mut audience: Vec<_> = terms.into_iter().filter(|term| term.service_grant().is_none()).cloned().collect();
        std::borrow::Cow::Owned(match audience.len() {
            0 => Self::Deny,
            1 => audience.pop().expect("one audience"),
            _ => Self::Or(audience),
        })
    }

    /// The device audience of `table_name`'s read rule: service grants
    /// removed, then every same-row disjunct a self-probe absorbs (a members
    /// row read under `.principal = viewer or exists members[.group, viewer]`
    /// is its own witness, so the first disjunct adds no reader). What
    /// remains must still be one of the three invertible forms.
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.000#3.1 — absorption is exact equivalence, never a narrowing.
    pub fn sync_audience_rule(&self, table_name: &str, table: &Table) -> std::borrow::Cow<'_, RuleExpr> {
        let audience = self.device_audience_rule();
        let terms = audience.disjuncts();
        if terms.len() < 2 { return audience; }
        let absorbed = |term: &RuleExpr| terms.iter().any(|other| crate::engine::rules::self_probe_absorbs(table_name, table, term, other));
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.000.000#2.2 — a repeated branch (in either equality order) is one audience.
        let mut kept: Vec<RuleExpr> = Vec::new();
        for term in terms.iter().filter(|term| !absorbed(term)) {
            let normal = term.normal_disjunct();
            if !kept.iter().any(|seen| seen.normal_disjunct() == normal) { kept.push((*term).clone()); }
        }
        if kept.len() == terms.len() { return audience; }
        std::borrow::Cow::Owned(if kept.len() == 1 { kept.pop().expect("one audience") } else { Self::Or(kept) })
    }

    /// A disjunct with `viewer = .col` spelled `.col = viewer`, for folding repeats.
    pub fn normal_disjunct(&self) -> RuleExpr {
        match self {
            Self::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, other) => Self::Eq(other.clone(), RuleTerm::Viewer),
            other => other.clone(),
        }
    }

    /// The rule as Quarry text, for diagnostics and rewrites.
    pub fn quarry(&self) -> String {
        fn term(term: &RuleTerm) -> String {
snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/schema/field_authority.rs:144:    pub fn references_field(&self, field: &str) -> bool {

//! Mutable syntax traversal shared by planning and final index-name resolution.
use crate::ast::*;

pub(crate) fn expression(value: &mut Expr, visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    match value {
        Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Exists { read, .. } | Expr::Created { read, .. } => read_children(read, visit),
        Expr::Unary { value, .. } | Expr::Member { base: value, .. } | Expr::Assert { value, .. } | Expr::ShapeUse { base: value, .. } => expression(value, visit),
        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => { expression(left, visit); expression(right, visit); }
        Expr::Array { items, .. } | Expr::Call { args: items, .. } => { for item in items { expression(item, visit); } }
        Expr::Object { fields, .. } => { for field in fields { if let Some(value) = &mut field.value { expression(value, visit); } } }
        Expr::Shape { base, fields, .. } => { expression(base, visit); shape(fields, visit); }
        Expr::If { then, condition, otherwise, .. } => { expression(then, visit); expression(condition, visit); expression(otherwise, visit); }
        Expr::Walk { seed, .. } => expression(seed, visit),
        Expr::Write { keys, fields, condition, .. } => {
            for key in keys { expression(&mut key.value, visit); }
            for field in fields { if let Some(value) = &mut field.value { expression(value, visit); } }
            if let Some(value) = condition { expression(value, visit); }
        }
        Expr::Merge(merge) => {
            for value in [&mut merge.matching, &mut merge.filter, &mut merge.cursor].into_iter().flatten() { expression(value, visit); }
            let mut lanes = Vec::new();
            for mut lane in std::mem::take(&mut merge.lanes) {
                if let Some(predicate) = &mut lane.admission { expression(predicate, visit); }
                if let Some((_, read)) = &mut lane.nulls {
                    let mut value = Expr::Read(*read.clone());
                    expression(&mut value, visit);
                    let Expr::Read(planned) = value else { unreachable!("planned null scan") };
                    **read = planned;
                }
                if let Some(value) = &mut lane.distinct { expression(value, visit); }
                if let Some(fields) = &mut lane.project { shape(fields, visit); }
                for value in &mut lane.prefix { expression(value, visit); }
                if let Some((_, value)) = &mut lane.loop_ { expression(value, visit); }
                if let Some(read) = &mut lane.planned {
                    let mut value = Expr::Read(*read.clone());
                    expression(&mut value, visit);
                    match value {
                        Expr::Read(planned) => **read = planned,
                        Expr::Merge(nested) if lane.loop_.is_none() => {
                            for mut child in nested.lanes {
                                child.branch = lane.branch.clone();
                                child.project = lane.project.clone();
                                lanes.push(child);
                            }
                            continue;
                        }
                        _ => unreachable!("a planned lane is a read or an expanded read"),
                    }
                }
                lanes.push(lane);
            }
            merge.lanes = lanes;
        }
        Expr::Name { .. } | Expr::Focal { .. } | Expr::Literal { .. } => {}
    }
    visit(value);
}

fn read_children(read: &mut ReadExpr, visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    for key in &mut read.keys { expression(&mut key.value, visit); }
    for seek in &mut read.seeks { expression(&mut seek.value, visit); }
    for value in [&mut read.filter, &mut read.matching].into_iter().flatten() { expression(value, visit); }
    if let Some(value) = read.range.as_mut().and_then(|range| range.cursor.as_mut()) { expression(value, visit); }
}

pub(super) fn shape(fields: &mut [ShapeField], visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    for field in fields { if let ShapeField::Field { value, .. } = field { expression(value, visit); } }
}

pub(super) fn statements(body: &mut [Stmt], visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    for stmt in body {
        match stmt {
            Stmt::Bind { value, .. } | Stmt::Expr { value, .. } | Stmt::Return { value, .. } => expression(value, visit),
            Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => expression(condition, visit),
            Stmt::Emit { payload, once, .. } => {
                expression(payload, visit);
                if let Some(key) = once { expression(key, visit); }
            }
            Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body, .. } => { expression(condition, visit); statements(then_body, visit); statements(else_body, visit); }
            Stmt::As { principal: domain, body, .. } | Stmt::For { domain, body, .. } => { expression(domain, visit); statements(body, visit); }
            Stmt::Break { .. } | Stmt::Continue { .. } => {}
        }
    }
}

pub(crate) fn document(doc: &mut Document, visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    for program in &mut doc.programs { statements(&mut program.prelude, visit); statements(&mut program.body, visit); }
    for function in &mut doc.functions {
        match &mut function.body { FunctionBody::Expression(value) => expression(value, visit), FunctionBody::Block(body) => statements(body, visit) }
    }
    for decl in &mut doc.shapes { shape(&mut decl.fields, visit); }
}
use super::*;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum RuleTerm {
    /// One authorized point read through a row-local typed reference.
    Reference { table: String, column: String, field: String, old: bool },
    /// Bounded live reads; a distinct variant makes old decoders refuse.
    ReferenceChain { column: String, hops: Vec<RuleHop>, old: bool },
    Field(String),
    OldField(String),
    NextField(String),
    Viewer,
    Literal(Value),
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct RuleHop { pub table: String, pub field: String }

pub const MAX_RULE_HOPS: usize = 3;

impl RuleTerm {
    pub fn reference_source(&self) -> Option<(&str, bool)> {
        match self {
            Self::Reference { column, old, .. } | Self::ReferenceChain { column, old, .. } => Some((column, *old)),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
    pub fn references(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&str, &str)> {
        let (one, many) = match self {
            Self::Reference { table, field, .. } => (Some((table.as_str(), field.as_str())), &[][..]),
            Self::ReferenceChain { hops, .. } => (None, hops.as_slice()),
            _ => (None, &[][..]),
        };
        one.into_iter().chain(many.iter().map(|hop| (hop.table.as_str(), hop.field.as_str())))
    }
}

impl RuleExpr {
    pub fn has_reference(&self) -> bool {
        self.any_term(&|t| t.reference_source().is_some())
    }
    pub fn any_term(&self, term: &impl Fn(&RuleTerm) -> bool) -> bool {
        match self {
            Self::Eq(a,b) | Self::Ne(a,b) | Self::Compare(_,a,b) => term(a) || term(b),
            Self::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | Self::Created { key, equalities, .. } => key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)|t)).any(term),
            Self::And(rs) | Self::Or(rs) => rs.iter().any(|r| r.any_term(term)),
            Self::Not(r) => r.any_term(term),
            _ => false,
        }
    }
}

pub(super) fn validate(schema: &Schema, source: &Table, term: &RuleTerm, update: bool, path: &str, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    let Some((column, old)) = term.reference_source() else { return; };
    let depth = term.references().count();
    let mut valid = !path.ends_with(".read") && (!old || update) && (1..=MAX_RULE_HOPS).contains(&depth)
        && !matches!(term, RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } if depth < 2);
    let (mut owner, mut column) = (source, column);
    for (table, field) in term.references() {
        valid &= schema.maintain_for(table).is_none()
            && matches!(owner.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required), Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: t }) if t == table)
            && !owner.maintained.contains_key(column) && !owner.written_by.get(column).is_some_and(|a| !a.audience);
        let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { valid = false; break; };
        valid &= target.ephemeral.is_none() && target.columns.contains_key(field)
            && !target.maintained.contains_key(field) && !target.written_by.get(field).is_some_and(|a| !a.audience);
        owner = target; column = field;
    }
    if !valid { issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.into(), message: "E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP: write rules allow up to three ordinary live references with audience-readable fields; use authorized mutation reads for other paths".into() }); }
}

pub(super) fn validate_term(schema: &Schema, table: &Table, term: &RuleTerm, update: bool, path: &str, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    match term {
        RuleTerm::Field(field) => {
            if !table.columns.contains_key(field) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("rule reads undeclared column {field}") });
            }
        }
        RuleTerm::OldField(field) | RuleTerm::NextField(field) => {
            if !update {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: "old and next images exist only in update rules".into() });
            } else if !table.columns.contains_key(field) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("rule reads undeclared column {field}") });
            }
        }
        RuleTerm::Reference { .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } => validate(schema, table, term, update, path, issues),
        RuleTerm::Viewer | RuleTerm::Literal(_) => {}
    }
}

codex
The review will be NOT READY. I found a false-refutation path in column observers, two gaps that can suppress role-escalation advice, and a helper guard propagation gap that can warn on an authorized admin workflow. I’ll include the source locations and repairs; I haven’t run tests.

codex
**NOT READY**

Four material defects found by inspection:

1. **Column observers can refute a policy that becomes Unknown before disclosure.**  
   [column_reads.rs:48](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-sec3/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/challenges/column_reads.rs:48) checks direct policy dependencies; [line 73](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-sec3/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/challenges/column_reads.rs:73) judges policy only before execution. For intent `.parent.owner = viewer`, a mutation can revoke the viewer’s access to the parent by changing its role roster, then return the protected column. The initial policy is false; the disclosure-time policy is Unknown. The observer nevertheless records a reachable refutation that fails check/test.  
   **Repair:** include transitive reference read-authority dependencies in mutation stability checks. Decline such observers until disclosure-boundary tracing establishes policy false at the actual observation.

2. **Role privileges encoded in probe keys are missed.**  
   [privilege.rs:7](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-sec3/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/privilege.rs:7) substitutes role tests only when the role literal appears in `equalities`. With a unique `(principal, role)` index, `exists roles[viewer, "admin"]` carries the role in `key`. That probe stays identical for every simulated caller/assigned role. Consequently, an open self-signup operation granting admin can produce no `N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION`.  
   **Repair:** map index key components to column names and normalize role constraints from both keys and extra equalities before comparing authority.

3. **An unrelated same-named argument overrides a literal role assignment.**  
   [lints.rs:125](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-sec3/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/lints.rs:125) falls back to the destination field name when the assigned expression is a literal, then replaces the target set with a matching argument’s enum variants. For example, an operation accepting `role` restricted to ordinary roles but explicitly inserting `role: "admin"` analyzes the ordinary argument choices instead of admin. This can suppress the required bootstrap warning.  
   **Repair:** narrow targets from an argument only when the resolved assigned expression actually references that argument. Preserve literal and default targets.

4. **Successful helper guards do not propagate to subsequent writes.**  
   [authority_walk.rs:18](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-sec3/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/authority_walk.rs:18) analyzes helper requirements in a cloned guard list and discards it on return. A mutation calling a helper that requires an operator service seat, then inserting a role, is analyzed without that dominating service guard and can receive an escalation warning. Moving the same requirement directly into the mutation changes the advice.  
   **Repair:** propagate requirements guaranteed on every successful helper return, or derive dominating guards from expanded bound control flow.

Read-only review of the current implementation and delta from `3653cae823`. No tests run, files modified, or qualification claim made.
